Attendance, daily reports, health details, pickup people, observations, invoices and
receipts all attach to the child record.
*Also called:* kid, student, pupil, little one, our loved one, key child.
*Where you see it:* Children.

**Children Attendance** — The page for marking **children** present, absent, late or
excused, for today only. A different page from Staff Attendance, with different options.
*Also called:* the register, taking the register, roll call, check-in, the morning register.
*Where you see it:* Children Attendance.

**Class** — A real room at your nursery. Belongs to one age group, can have several teachers
assigned, and carries its own daily-report settings.
*Also called:* classroom, room, group, base room.
*Where you see it:* Classes.

**Comment** — On a daily report, the free-text box for activities and behaviour. On an
assessment report, the written text you add for each child.
*Also called:* notes, remarks, write-up.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports; Assessment Report.

**CRM** — Here, **parents enquiring about a nursery place**. This is the point worth being
clear about: Peekaboo's CRM is admissions, not sales. There are no deals, no quotes, no
revenue pipeline and no sales targets. When someone says "check the CRM", they mean the
enquiry list.
*Also called:* admissions, leads, enquiries, the waiting list, the pipeline.
*Where you see it:* Leads; the CRM tab of the dashboard.

**Curriculum** — The early-years framework your nursery assesses against, and the top of the
whole learning structure. It owns your age groups and your attainment scale, and contains
the learning areas.
*Also called:* framework, scheme of work, the curriculum model.
*Where you see it:* Curriculum.

**Daily Care** — The section of a daily report holding water, juice, medicine and hygiene as
free-text boxes.
*Also called:* care notes, personal care.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Daily Report** — The care record for one child on one day that goes home to the family:
mood, meals, nap, milk, items needed, and the note to parents. Saved as a draft, then
published, and emailed to the parent.
*Also called:* journal, day sheet, care report, diary, daily sheet, what they did today.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Dashboard** — See **Live Dashboard**.

**Dietary** — A type of health record used for food restrictions, intolerances and special
diets. This is where special diets belong; there is no separate dietary screen.
*Also called:* special diet, food requirement, intolerance, food plan.
*Where you see it:* Children, in the Health section of a child's record.

**Draft** — A daily report that has been saved but not yet published, so no parent can see
it.
*Also called:* unpublished, work in progress, not sent yet.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Early Childhood** — The sidebar group covering classes, curriculum, age groups, attainment,
learning areas, assessments, observations and reports.
*Also called:* curriculum module, learning, EC, the assessment side.
*Where you see it:* sidebar group.

**Early years** — The industry term for childcare and education from birth to around five —
the whole world Peekaboo is built for.
*Also called:* preschool, foundation stage, under-fives.

**Enquiry** — A family's first contact about a place. Stored as a lead.
*Also called:* lead, prospect, enquiry, application, the family who rang.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Evaluation** — An admissions stage between application and acceptance, covering assessing
the child's needs or readiness and issuing the offer.
*Also called:* assessment stage, offer letter, review.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Excused** — An attendance status for **children only**, meaning an authorised absence — a
planned holiday, a medical appointment, a family event. The staff equivalent is **On Leave**.
*Also called:* authorised absence, approved absence, holiday, planned absence.
*Where you see it:* Children Attendance; Attendance Reports.

**Expense** — A cost the nursery has incurred, linked to a supplier and an expense type, with
the receipt or invoice scan attached. The one page in Money Out built around proof of spend.
*Also called:* cost, spend, outgoing, bill, disbursement.
*Where you see it:* Expenses.

**Expense Type** — A spending category such as Cleaning, Utilities or Stationery. At least
one must exist before an expense can be saved. On the Expenses table this appears under the
column heading **Supply**.
*Also called:* cost category, expense category, spending category, nominal code.
*Where you see it:* Expense Types; Expenses.

**Grade** — On an admissions enquiry, the class or level the family is asking about.
*Also called:* year, level, class, age band.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Health record** — A structured entry on a child's record describing a medical matter, with
a type such as Allergy, Chronic or Dietary. Recording an allergy this way is what produces
the red allergy warning on the register.
*Also called:* medical record, allergy record, condition, medical note.
*Where you see it:* Children, in the Health section of a child's record.

**Health Status** — A set of buttons on the daily-report form used while filling it in. See
the note under **Note to parents** — anything about a child's health belongs in the note
box and in a conversation with your room lead.
*Also called:* wellbeing, how they were.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Hygiene** — A free-text box in the Daily Care section of a daily report.
*Also called:* toileting, nappy notes, personal care.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Inactive** — A record kept for its history but no longer in everyday use. This is
Peekaboo's version of archiving, and it is the right way to handle a child who has left or an
employee who has departed. It can always be switched back. On My Center's sessions table the
same idea reads **Not Active**.
*Also called:* archived, closed, left, former, disabled, retired.
*Where you see it:* Children; Staff; Classes; Curriculum; Learn Area; My Center; Roles &
Access.

**INV** — The sky-blue badge on every invoice card. It is a document label meaning "this is
an invoice", not a status, and it carries no payment information.
*Also called:* the invoice tag.
*Where you see it:* Invoices.

**Invoice** — A bill raised against a **child** for one or more services. Invoices have no
paid or unpaid marker; to see where a family stands, use Balance by Class.
*Also called:* bill, fee note, charge, statement, fee invoice.
*Where you see it:* Invoices.

**Items Needed** — The daily-report section with yes/no toggles for **Diapers**, **Clothes**,
**Wipes** and **Cream**, telling the parent what to send in.
*Also called:* supplies needed, please send, restock request.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Late** — An attendance status meaning the person attended but arrived after the expected
start. Available for both children and staff. On the dashboard a late child still counts as
present.
*Also called:* late arrival, tardy, delayed.
*Where you see it:* Children Attendance; Staff Attendance; Attendance Reports; Live
Dashboard.

**Lead** — One prospective family's enquiry, held as a single record carrying both the
child's and the parent's details. A lead is not connected to a child record — when a family
joins, the child is created separately.
*Also called:* enquiry, prospect, applicant, application, potential family.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Learn Area / Learning Area** — A broad area of learning within a curriculum, containing
aspects. On the printed assessment report a learning area may be called a *main domain*.
*Also called:* area of learning, domain, main domain, subject, strand.
*Where you see it:* Learn Area; Assessment Report.

**Live Dashboard** — The page you land on after signing in. Three tabs of read-only figures
and charts: **Main Dashboard**, **Finance** and **CRM**. The only page that refreshes itself.
*Also called:* dashboard, home, overview, the front page.
*Where you see it:* Live Dashboard.

**Lost** — The admissions stage meaning the family will not be joining because **they**
withdrew, went elsewhere or went quiet. Distinct from Rejected, where the nursery declined.
*Also called:* dropped, gone cold, withdrawn, not proceeding.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Media** — A message type for sending photos to families.
*Also called:* photos, pictures, gallery.
*Where you see it:* Messages.

**Messages** — The one-way noticeboard publishing announcements, newsletters, photos and
videos to parents through the separate parent app. No inbox, no replies, no read receipts.
*Also called:* announcements, newsletter, broadcast, parent comms, the timeline, the
newsfeed.
*Where you see it:* Messages.

**Mood** — The single overall impression of a child's day, chosen under the heading **Today
I Was** on the daily report.
*Also called:* how they were, temperament, spirits.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**My Center** — Your **own** nursery's settings: name, address, phone numbers, email, logo
and session times. This is where you change your nursery's details — never Centers.
*Also called:* our details, nursery settings, site settings, our profile.
*Where you see it:* My Center.

**Nap** — The daily-report section recording whether and how long a child slept, with
**From** and **To** times.
*Also called:* sleep, rest, quiet time, siesta.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Nappy** — Recorded through the **Diapers** toggle in Items Needed and the **Hygiene**
free-text box. There is no separate structured nappy log.
*Also called:* diaper, toileting, changing, bathroom, potty.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**New Lead** — The admissions stage for a brand-new enquiry that nobody has worked yet. The
top of the queue.
*Also called:* new enquiry, fresh lead, uncontacted.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**NOS** — The small grey label that sits above most page titles, and the version badge in
the sidebar header. It is product branding rather than a feature, and nothing depends on it.
*Where you see it:* sidebar header; above most page titles.

**Not Active** — The wording used on the sessions table on My Center for a session no longer
offered. It means the same as **Inactive**.
*Also called:* inactive, switched off, not offered.
*Where you see it:* My Center.

**Not Marked** — The state of a person whose attendance has not been recorded yet today. It
does **not** mean absent — it means nobody has said anything yet. Shown as a hyphen in
Attendance Reports.
*Also called:* unmarked, blank, nothing recorded, not taken.
*Where you see it:* Children Attendance; Staff Attendance; Attendance Reports.

**Note to parents** — The free-text box on the daily report where you write directly to the
family. **This is where illness, injury and temperature readings belong**, because free text
is saved, published and sent exactly as you wrote it.
*Also called:* teacher note, message home, comments to parent, the note.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports.

**Objective** — An individual learning goal a child is assessed against, reached by combining
an aspect with an age group. Objectives are chosen and rated when recording an observation.
*Also called:* goal, milestone, outcome, target, learning goal, next step.
*Where you see it:* the objective selector inside Observations.

**Observation** — A record that a named teacher watched a named child on a date, tagged with
the objectives it demonstrated, each rated at an attainment level. Observations are the raw
material of assessment reports.
*Also called:* learning story, obs, evidence, wow moment, note, snapshot.
*Where you see it:* Observations.

**On Leave** — An attendance status for **staff only**, meaning authorised time off —
holiday, approved absence, sick leave. The children's equivalent is **Excused**. The quick
button reads just **Leave**.
*Also called:* leave, holiday, annual leave, time off, booked off.
*Where you see it:* Staff Attendance; Attendance Reports.

**Operator** — On an admissions enquiry, the staff member who created or owns it.
*Also called:* owner, handled by, assigned to, our contact.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Our Heroes** — Peekaboo's own name for **staff**. You will meet it as a tile label when an
administrator sets up what a role can open.
*Also called:* staff, employees, team, the team.
*Where you see it:* Roles & Access.

**Our Loved Ones** — Peekaboo's own name for **children**. Like Our Heroes, it appears as a
tile label when setting up role access.
*Also called:* children, kids, students, the little ones.
*Where you see it:* Roles & Access.

**Package** — Your **nursery's own subscription to the Peekaboo software**, bought by card.
Nothing to do with billing parents.
*Also called:* subscription, plan, licence, seats.
*Where you see it:* Packages.

**Parent** — The account holder a child is attached to, and the person who receives daily
reports and messages. Parents use a **separate app** and cannot sign into this portal.
*Also called:* guardian, mum, dad, family, carer, account holder.
*Where you see it:* Children, in the Parent section of a child's record.

**Payment Mode** — A way money moves — cash, cheque, bank transfer, card — each carrying the
accounting codes that post into your ledger. Needed on both receipts and payment vouchers.
*Also called:* payment method, tender type, how they paid.
*Where you see it:* Payment Modes.

**Payment Voucher** — A record of money actually leaving the bank to a supplier, which can be
split across several payment lines.
*Also called:* PV, payout, remittance, supplier payment, cheque run.
*Where you see it:* Payment Voucher.

**Pickup person** — A named adult authorised to collect a child. A child can have any number.
*Also called:* authorised collector, collection list, who can collect, nominated adult.
*Where you see it:* Children, in the Pickup section of a child's record.

**Present** — An attendance status meaning the person is in today and has been checked in.
Available for both children and staff.
*Also called:* in, here, attended, on site.
*Where you see it:* Children Attendance; Staff Attendance; Attendance Reports.

**Principal** — The role that runs the nursery and sees the whole portal, including the
multi-nursery **Centers** screen.
*Also called:* head, director, owner, manager, nursery manager.
*Where you see it:* the role badge in the top bar.

**Published** — Used two ways. A **daily report** that has been marked finished. A **message**
that is live on the parents' timeline, shown by a green dot.
*Also called:* sent, released, live, out.
*Where you see it:* Daily Reports; Messages.

**Purchase** — A record of what was ordered from a supplier, itemised line by line with
quantities, fees and VAT.
*Also called:* purchase order, PO, order, goods received.
*Where you see it:* Purchases.

**Received** — On Balance by Class, the total of everything receipted for a child. Shown in
red with a `-`.
*Also called:* paid, collected, money in, takings.
*Where you see it:* Balance by Class.

**Receipt** — A record of money received from a family, raised against a **child** and
settled across one or more payment lines. Receipts are not attached to particular invoices.
*Also called:* payment, fee received, money in, proof of payment.
*Where you see it:* Receipts.

**Referral** — On an enquiry, **how the family heard about you** — a campaign, a sign, a
recommendation. Required on every enquiry.
*Also called:* referred by, marketing channel, how they found us, word of mouth.
*Where you see it:* Referrals & Sources; the enquiry form.

**Registered** — The final admissions stage, meaning the child is registered and the enquiry
is complete. Reaching it does not create a child record — that step is done by hand.
*Also called:* enrolled, signed up, joined, confirmed.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Registrar** — The front-desk role: enquiries, tours, enrolments, and keeping child and
parent records current.
*Also called:* admissions officer, front desk, receptionist, the office.
*Where you see it:* the role badge in the top bar.

**Registration** — **The link between a child and a class**, tying together the child, the
parent, the class, the session, a date range and the chosen weekdays. Moving a child to
another room, or changing their days or session, is an edit to the registration. A child with
no registration appears in no classroom.
*Also called:* enrolment, enrollment, placement, class assignment, booking, the child's place.
*Where you see it:* Children, in the Registrations section of a child's record.

**Rejected** — The admissions stage meaning **the nursery** turned the family down. Distinct
from Lost, where the family withdrew.
*Also called:* declined, turned down, unsuccessful.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**Role** — A job title that carries a set of areas its holders may open. Roles are created
and configured on Roles & Access; each staff member is given one on the Staff page.
*Also called:* permission group, user type, access level, job role.
*Where you see it:* Roles & Access; the staff form; the role badge in the top bar.

**Roles & Access** — The administrator's page for creating roles and ticking which areas each
may open.
*Also called:* permissions, access control, user rights, security settings.
*Where you see it:* Roles & Access.

**Service** — A billable catalogue item — tuition, transport, uniform, lunch plan — with a
default fee, default VAT and a tax-exempt setting. At least one must exist before an invoice
can be raised.
*Also called:* fee, charge, price list item, tariff, billing item.
*Where you see it:* Services.

**Session** — A daily time band such as morning, afternoon or full day, defined for your
nursery and required on every registration.
*Also called:* time slot, shift, half day, session time, timing.
*Where you see it:* My Center; the Registrations section of a child's record.

**Source** — On an enquiry, **which channel the enquiry arrived through**. Paired with
Referral, and also required on every enquiry.
*Also called:* communication source, channel, record source, how it came in.
*Where you see it:* Referrals & Sources; the enquiry form.

**Staff** — An employee record, carrying their contact details, role and portal login.
*Also called:* employee, teacher, team member, colleague, our heroes.
*Where you see it:* Staff.

**Staff Attendance** — The page for clocking **employees** in and out, for today only. A
different page from Children Attendance, with different options.
*Also called:* clock in, timesheet, staff sign-in, staff register, the signing-in book.
*Where you see it:* Staff Attendance.

**Supplier** — A vendor your nursery buys from or pays out to. Required on every expense,
purchase and payment voucher.
*Also called:* vendor, contractor, provider, trader.
*Where you see it:* Suppliers.

**Supply** — The column heading on the Expenses table that shows the **expense type**. Treat
"Supply" and "Expense Type" as the same thing on that page.
*Where you see it:* Expenses.

**Tax exempt** — A setting marking an item as not subject to VAT. On a service it marks the
item VAT-free; on an invoice or purchase it sets every line's VAT to zero.
*Also called:* VAT exempt, zero rated, no tax, non-vatable.
*Where you see it:* Services; Invoices; Purchases.

**Teacher** — The role that runs a room: the register, daily reports, observations and
assessment reports.
*Also called:* educator, practitioner, class teacher, key person.
*Where you see it:* the role badge in the top bar; the teacher assignment on Classes.

**Tour** — A family's visit to the nursery, recorded as an admissions stage plus its
follow-up task. There is no separate tour booking screen — capture the date and time in the
task.
*Also called:* visit, show-round, viewing, open day, look round.
*Where you see it:* Leads.

**VAT** — Value Added Tax, held as a default percentage on each service and totalled on
invoices and purchases.
*Also called:* tax, sales tax, output tax.
*Where you see it:* Services; Invoices; Purchases.

**Video Media** — A message type for sending a video to families. Videos may be up to 35 MB.
*Also called:* video, clip, film.
*Where you see it:* Messages.

## Abbreviations you will meet

**AED** — The currency Peekaboo works in, shown on Balance by Class. *Also called:* dirhams.

**CRM** — Here, admissions and parent enquiries. See the full entry above. It does **not**
mean a sales system.

**CSV** — A spreadsheet file format used by the export buttons. Opens in Excel or any
spreadsheet program. *Also called:* spreadsheet, export file.

**DOB** — Date of birth. *Where you see it:* Leads; the child form.

**EC** — Early Childhood, the curriculum and assessment group in the sidebar.

**INV** — The document badge on invoice cards. See the full entry above.

**MOQ** — Minimum order quantity, a field on a service record.

**PDF** — The printable document format. Most **PDF** and **Print** buttons open a print
preview; choose "Save as PDF" in your browser's print dialog to get a file.

**PV** — Payment voucher. See the full entry above.

**QB Account** — A QuickBooks account reference on a payment mode. Your accountant supplies
the correct value.

**QTY** — Quantity, a column on invoice and purchase lines.

**RV** — The label shown on a receipt's badge when no receipt type has been set. A document
marker, not a status.

**VAT** — Value Added Tax. See the full entry above.

---

# Part B — Statuses

Every status and label the portal shows you, grouped by where you meet it.

For each one: what it means, when it appears, what normally happens next, and whether you
need to do anything.

---

## Children's attendance

Marked on **Children Attendance**, for today only. Remember these four are for children —
staff have a slightly different set.

### Present
**What it means:** The child is in the nursery today and has been checked in.
**When it appears:** When you click **Present** on the child's row, or choose it in **Edit**.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** An arrival time is stamped automatically if one was not already there.
At the end of the day a **Checkout** button appears on the row so you can record their
departure.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — click it as each child arrives.** Nothing marks a
child present on its own.

### Absent
**What it means:** The child did not attend today, and the absence was not arranged in
advance.
**When it appears:** When you click **Absent**, or choose it in **Edit**.
**Colour:** Red.
**What happens next:** Both the arrival and departure times are cleared, which is correct —
the child was not here.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — record why in the Notes box inside Edit.** There is
no reason dropdown, so the note is the record. If you later switch the status back, re-enter
the times by hand, since they were cleared.

### Late
**What it means:** The child is here, but arrived after the expected start time.
**When it appears:** When you click **Late**.
**Colour:** Amber.
**What happens next:** An arrival time is stamped if one was not already there. The child
appears in the **Late Arrivals** panel on the **Live Dashboard**, with your note shown as the
reason — or **No reason given** if you left the note blank. A late child counts as present in
the dashboard's **Children Present** figure, since they are in the building.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — put the reason in Notes**, so the dashboard shows
something useful.

### Excused
**What it means:** The child is away and the absence was authorised — a planned holiday, a
medical appointment, a family event.
**When it appears:** When you click **Excused**.
**Colour:** Blue.
**What happens next:** Arrival and departure times are cleared, as with Absent. On Attendance
Reports, excused days are included in the **Total Absent Days** figure alongside absent and
leave days.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — note the authorisation in Notes**, so the record
shows the absence was agreed.
**Note:** Excused is for **children only**. For staff, use **On Leave**.

### Not Marked
**What it means:** Nobody has recorded anything for this child today. **It does not mean
absent.**
**When it appears:** Automatically, on every child's row at the start of each day, until
somebody marks them.
**Colour:** Grey.
**What happens next:** Nothing at all, unless a person acts. No reminder is sent and nothing
runs overnight.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — this is the most important action on the page.**
Unmarked children are counted in neither the present nor the absent totals, which is why
dashboard figures sometimes do not add up to the full roll. Clearing every **Not Marked**
before the morning is out is the single best habit in this module.
**Note:** Once a status has been saved there is no way to return a child to Not Marked. Pick
the status that fits rather than trying to undo.

---

## Staff attendance

Marked on **Staff Attendance**, for today only.

### Present
**What it means:** The employee has clocked in today.
**When it appears:** When you click **Check in**, or choose Present in **Edit**.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** **Check in** is replaced by **Checkout**. Pressing Checkout at the end
of the shift stamps the departure time and keeps the status as it was.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — check each employee in.**
**Note:** Once an arrival time exists the **Check in** button does not come back. To adjust
the time, use **Edit**.

### Absent
**What it means:** The employee did not attend, and was not on authorised leave.
**When it appears:** When you click **Absent**, or choose it in **Edit**.
**Colour:** Red.
**What happens next:** The quick button clears both times. The edit dialog leaves the times
as you have set them, so if you want a clean absent record the quick button is the tidier
route.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes, mark it. There is no leave-request link and no approval
step here — this is a record of the day, not a request.

### Late
**What it means:** The employee attended but arrived after their scheduled start.
**When it appears:** **Only from the Edit dialog.** There is no quick button for Late on this
page — open **Edit**, choose **Late** from the status dropdown, and save.
**Colour:** Amber.
**What happens next:** Nothing automatic. The portal does not accumulate lateness totals or
calculate hours worked.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes, and note the extra step: staff lateness takes two clicks
where a child's takes one.

### On Leave
**What it means:** The employee is away on authorised leave — holiday, approved time off,
sickness leave.
**When it appears:** When you click the quick button reading **Leave**, or choose **On Leave**
in **Edit**. The summary card reads **On Leave**. All three refer to the same thing.
**Colour:** Blue.
**What happens next:** The quick button clears both times. On Attendance Reports, leave days
are included in the **Total Absent Days** figure alongside absent and excused days.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes, mark it. Approving leave itself happens through your own
process outside the portal.
**Note:** On Leave is for **staff only**. For children, use **Excused**.

### Not Marked
**What it means:** Nothing has been recorded for this employee today.
**When it appears:** Automatically each day until somebody marks them.
**Colour:** Grey.
**What happens next:** Nothing.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — clock them in, or mark them Absent or Leave.
**Note:** If you open **Edit** on an unmarked row, the status dropdown starts at **Present**
as a helpful default. Change it if that is not what you mean.

---

## Attendance report letters

These appear on **Attendance Reports**, one small lettered circle per person per day. The
page is read-only — letters cannot be clicked or changed.

### P
**What it means:** Present that day. Hovering shows **Present**.
**When it appears:** When present was recorded for that person and date.
**Colour:** Solid green circle.
**What happens next:** Counts towards **Total Present Days** and the present percentage.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.

### A
**What it means:** Absent that day. Hovering shows **Absent**.
**When it appears:** When absent was recorded.
**Colour:** Red outline, pale red fill.
**What happens next:** Counts towards **Total Absent Days**, a figure that also includes
leave and excused days.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.

### L
**What it means:** Arrived late that day. Hovering shows **Late**.
**When it appears:** When late was recorded.
**Colour:** Amber outline, pale amber fill.
**What happens next:** Counts towards **Total Late Days**. Note that on this page late is
counted separately from present, whereas the dashboard folds late into its present figure.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.

### V
**What it means:** On authorised leave. Hovering shows **Leave**. Appears on staff rows.
**When it appears:** When leave was recorded on Staff Attendance.
**Colour:** Blue outline, pale blue fill.
**What happens next:** Counted inside **Total Absent Days**.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.
**Note:** The letter **V** is used because **L** is already taken by Late.

### E
**What it means:** An authorised absence. Hovering shows **Excused**. Appears on children's
rows.
**When it appears:** When excused was recorded on Children Attendance.
**Colour:** Blue outline, pale blue fill — very similar to **V**.
**What happens next:** Counted inside **Total Absent Days**.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.
**Worth knowing:** **E** is not listed in the on-screen legend, so it is worth remembering:
on a **child's** row, blue means **Excused**; on a **staff** row, blue means **Leave**.

### – (a hyphen)
**What it means:** Not marked. Nothing was recorded for that person on that date.
**When it appears:** Whenever no record was saved.
**Colour:** Grey outline, no fill.
**What happens next:** Counts towards **Total Not Marked Days**, but only on weekdays —
Saturdays and Sundays still show as columns but are left out of the totals. Public holidays
count as ordinary working days.
**Do you need to do anything?** Not for that date — past registers cannot be corrected
anywhere in the portal. Treat a run of hyphens as a prompt to tighten the daily routine.

**In exports:** the CSV and print versions show the full word — Present, Absent, Late, Leave,
Excused, Not marked — rather than the letter, which makes exported files easier to share.

---

## Active and Inactive

The same pair appears across the portal — on children, staff, classes, curricula, learning
areas, sessions and roles.

### Active
**What it means:** The record is in use and appears in dropdowns and default lists.
**When it appears:** On creation. **Everything starts Active.**
**Colour:** Green in most places.
**What happens next:** The record shows up wherever it is needed — active classes appear in
the class dropdown when registering a child, active sessions appear in the timing dropdown,
active classes appear as filter chips on Balance by Class.
**Do you need to do anything?** No. Active is the default and is usually what you want.

### Inactive
**What it means:** The record is kept for its history but should no longer be used in
everyday work. **This is Peekaboo's version of archiving.**
**When it appears:** When somebody deliberately switches the status off.
**Colour:** Usually red or grey, depending on the page. On **Curriculum** and **Learn Area**
the badge displays in green whatever the setting, so on those two pages **read the word
rather than the colour**.
**What happens next:** The record disappears from dropdowns where a live choice is needed —
classes, sessions — but stays in list views unless you filter it out. Nothing is deleted.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes, deliberately: **this is the right way to handle a child
who has left or an employee who has departed.** It keeps their history intact and can be
switched back at any time.
**Two practical notes:** On the **Children** page, the **Clear** button resets search, class
and age filters but leaves the status filter where you set it — set it back to **All Status**
by hand. And on **Staff** and **Roles**, the one-click status button flips immediately with
no confirmation, so click it deliberately.

### Not Active
**What it means:** Exactly the same as Inactive — the wording just differs on this one table.
**When it appears:** On the sessions table on **My Center**.
**Colour:** Grey.
**What happens next:** The session disappears from the timing dropdown when registering a
child.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes if somebody reports an empty timing dropdown while
registering a child — that is the usual cause. Switch a session back on, or create one.

---

## Admissions enquiry stages

These appear on **Leads** as the coloured badge on each row. Below they are listed in the
order a family actually travels through them.

### Where to look for the authoritative stage

Before the list, one point that will save you real confusion.

**The badge on the Leads page is the authoritative stage.** Some stages appear under
different wording on the **Live Dashboard**, and under slightly different wording again in the
menu you use to move an enquiry along. This shows up most around the tour stages, where the
dashboard and the Leads page use different words for the same point in the journey.

Two practical consequences:

- **When you need to know where one family stands, read the badge on the Leads page.** Use the
  dashboard for the shape of the month — are enquiries up, are we converting — and the Leads
  page for the individual answer.
- **If you move an enquiry using a menu option and the badge afterwards reads differently,
  the enquiry has moved correctly.** The stage is right; the two screens simply name it
  differently. Nothing has gone wrong and you do not need to redo it.

Also worth knowing: **read the stage by its word, not its colour.** Only one stage is red, and
two rather different outcomes share a similar blue, so the badge text is the reliable signal.

### New Lead
**What it means:** A brand-new enquiry that nobody has worked yet.
**When it appears:** When an enquiry is added, and as the starting point for any enquiry whose
stage is not recognised.
**Colour:** Blue.
**What happens next:** Somebody calls or emails the family and arranges a visit, moving the
enquiry to **Book a Tour**.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes.** This is the top of the queue — an enquiry sitting
here has had no contact at all.

### Book a Tour
**What it means:** A nursery visit is being arranged for the family. **The visit has not
happened yet.**
**When it appears:** When you agree a visit with the parent and set this stage.
**Colour:** Amber.
**What happens next:** After the visit, the enquiry moves on to **KYC** and then through the
application stages.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes.** Amber means an appointment is pending. Capture the
date and time in the follow-up task — there is no separate tour booking screen, so the task is
where the details live.

### KYC
**What it means:** "Know Your Customer" — the information-gathering step, and in most
nurseries' use the point at which the family has visited and details are being collected and
checked.
**When it appears:** When you set this stage, generally after the tour.
**Colour:** Blue.
**What happens next:** The enquiry moves on to **Admission**, or to **Evaluation**.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — and agree with your team what this stage means for
you.** Nurseries use it slightly differently. Write your own definition down so everyone reads
the badge the same way.

### Evaluation
**What it means:** The assessment stage — looking at the child's needs or readiness, and the
point at which an offer is issued.
**When it appears:** When you set this stage.
**Colour:** Blue.
**What happens next:** The family moves on to **Accepted**, or the enquiry closes as
**Rejected** or **Lost**.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — whatever your evaluation process requires.

### Admission
**What it means:** The family is proceeding into the admission process proper — the
application stage, with paperwork to complete.
**When it appears:** When the family decides to apply.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** Evaluation, then acceptance.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes** — the admission paperwork needs chasing.

### Accepted
**What it means:** Your nursery has accepted the child.
**When it appears:** When you set this stage, or when you press the **Approve** button in the
enquiry's detail header.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** The family completes registration, and ideally the enquiry reaches
**Registered**.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes, and this is the most important action item in the whole
module. Acceptance is not enrolment.** No child record is created by this stage. Open the
**Children** page and create the child properly — name, birth date, parent, health details,
pickup people, and the registration that puts them in a room. A good standing rule is: *when a
lead reaches Accepted, create the child record the same day.*
**Note:** Once an enquiry is Accepted, the **Approve** button reads **Approved** and locks.
Moving the stage from the badge menu unlocks it again.

### Registered
**What it means:** The child is registered — the end of the happy path within admissions.
**When it appears:** **Set from the task dialog rather than the badge menu.** Open the
enquiry, press **+** in the Tasks column, and set the stage there.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** Nothing further within admissions. The enquiry stays in the list as the
historical record of how the family joined.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes, outside admissions** — if you have not already created
the child record on the **Children** page, do it now.

### Lost
**What it means:** The family will not be joining, because **they** withdrew, went elsewhere or
went quiet.
**When it appears:** When you set this stage.
**Colour:** Red. This is the only red stage.
**What happens next:** Nothing. The enquiry stays fully visible in the list.
**Do you need to do anything?** Not strictly, but **record why** — in the comment box when you
change the stage, or as a **Note**. Six months on, the reason is the valuable part.

### Rejected
**What it means:** **The nursery** turned the family down, as opposed to Lost, where the family
withdrew.
**When it appears:** When an admissions decision goes against the applicant.
**Colour:** Blue — similar to **New Lead**, so read the word rather than the colour.
**What happens next:** Nothing. The enquiry stays in the list.
**Do you need to do anything?** No, though a **Note** explaining the decision is good practice.

**Two general notes on stages.** Any stage can be set at any time — there is no locked sequence,
so you can correct a mistake by simply setting the right one. And changing a stage creates a
follow-up task and asks for a reminder date; treat that as your prompt to decide when you will
next contact the family, and keep your own diary, because nothing in the portal will chase you
on the day.

---

## Daily report states

Shown on each child's card in the **Daily Reports** list, and counted in the header line.

### Published
**What it means:** The report has been marked as finished.
**When it appears:** When you press **Publish** or **Publish & Email**.
**Colour:** Green.
**What happens next:** **Publishing on its own does not email anybody.** This is the single
most useful thing to know about the page.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes, if the family is to receive it.** Use **Publish &
Email**, or press the envelope button on the card afterwards.
**Note:** Reports are not locked after publishing. The note to parents can still be edited
with the pencil icon.

### Draft
**What it means:** A report exists and has been saved, but has not been marked finished, so no
parent can see it.
**When it appears:** When you press **Save Draft**.
**Colour:** Amber.
**What happens next:** Nothing automatic. The draft waits for you and can be reopened.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — publish it when it is complete.
**Note:** Finish and publish a report in one sitting where you can. Drafts are for building the
report across the day, not for carrying over to tomorrow.

### Report not created
**What it means:** No daily report has been written for this child on this date.
**When it appears:** As the starting state for every child each day.
**Colour:** Red.
**What happens next:** Nothing. The envelope button on the card stays unavailable, with the
tooltip **Report not created yet**.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — select the child and press **Create Report**.

**One line that is not a status:** the footer of a report always reads **Parent has not viewed
yet**. It is not a read receipt and it does not change. If you need to know that a parent has
seen something, ask them.

---

## Daily report — mood

Chosen under the heading **Today I Was**. Five buttons, and not required — though it is worth
picking one.

| Label | What it means |
|---|---|
| **Great** | A really good day |
| **Good** | A settled, positive day |
| **Okay** | A steady day, nothing remarkable |
| **Unsettled** | Out of sorts, restless or clingy |
| **Upset** | A difficult day |

**When it appears:** As soon as you click one of the five icons.
**What happens next:** The mood appears on the report the parent reads.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — choose one deliberately.** A report left untouched
saves with a neutral, positive mood, so an unsettled day will not show as unsettled unless you
say so.
**Worth knowing:** **Great**, **Unsettled** and **Upset** are the three that read most clearly
back to a parent. If a child's day was genuinely difficult, pick **Unsettled** or **Upset**
*and* explain it in the **Note to parents** box — the written note is what carries the detail.

---

## Daily report — meals

Four buttons for each meal. The meals are **Breakfast**, **Snack AM**, **Lunch**, **Snack PM**
and **Milk**.

| Label | What it means | Colour when chosen |
|---|---|---|
| **All** | Ate everything offered | Green |
| **Most** | Ate the greater part | Blue |
| **Some** | Ate a little | Amber |
| **None** | Did not eat | Red |

**When it appears:** As soon as you click a button for that meal.
**What happens next:** The amount appears on the parent's report beside the meal, along with
anything you wrote in the comment box and the time.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — use the comment box beside each meal.** This is the
habit that makes meal records genuinely useful.

A short written note is always clearer than a button on its own, and it is especially worth
doing in two situations:

- **When a child refuses a meal.** Write it: *"Refused lunch, ate a little fruit at 13:15."*
  That sentence tells the parent far more than a button, and it is unmistakable.
- **For milk feeds.** Record the amount and the time in the comment box beside milk — that
  written detail is what a parent of a baby most wants to read.

The same applies to the afternoon snack: note it in the comment box, and the parent has the
full picture of the day.

---

## Daily report — nap

Four options, with **From** and **To** time pickers appearing when you choose one of the first
two.

| Label | What it means |
|---|---|
| **Slept Well** | A proper, settled sleep |
| **Brief Nap** | A short doze |
| **No Nap** | Awake all day |
| **N/A** | Not applicable for this child |

**When it appears:** As soon as you click one of the four.
**What happens next:** The nap details appear on the parent's report.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — always fill in the From and To times whenever a child
sleeps at all.** The times are the part of the nap record that carries through most reliably,
and they are what a parent actually plans their evening around. Even a twenty-minute doze is
worth its two timestamps.

If a child did not sleep, say so in the **Note to parents** box — *"No nap today, was happy and
busy all afternoon"* — so the parent knows it was noticed rather than forgotten.

---

## Daily report — items needed

Four yes/no toggles: **Diapers**, **Clothes**, **Wipes** and **Cream**.

**What they mean:** Whether you need the parent to send more of that item.
**When they appear:** On every report, both set to no until you choose.
**Colour:** **Yes** turns green, **No** turns red.
**What happens next:** The requests appear on the parent's report.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — tap **Yes** for anything genuinely running low. An item
left untouched is reported as not needed, so a request only reaches the parent if you make it.

---

## Daily report — health and temperature

**Health Status** offers **Healthy**, **Mild Concern** and **Sent Home**, and there is a
temperature box that flags a reading of 38°C or above on screen as you type it. These are
working aids while you complete the form, and **these two selections are not retained on the
saved report.**

So whenever a child is unwell, has been hurt, or has a raised temperature, follow this
procedure:

1. **Write it in the Note to parents box.** State what happened, when, what you did, and the
   actual temperature reading if one was taken. For example: *"Ayesha felt warm after lunch.
   Temperature taken at 13:40 was 38.2°C. She was kept cool and quiet, drank water, and we
   called you at 14:00."* Free text like this is saved, published and sent to the parent
   exactly as you wrote it.
2. **Tell the room lead in person, straight away.** No screen in Peekaboo alerts anybody — not
   the office, not the principal, not the parent. Nothing in the portal replaces speaking to a
   colleague about an unwell child.
3. **For anything ongoing** — an allergy, asthma, a dietary requirement — also record it on the
   child's **Health** section on the **Children** page, so that it travels with the child every
   day rather than sitting on a single report.

This is simply the correct way to record illness, injury and temperature. Write it down, and
say it out loud.

---

## Calendar event types

Events have no status — the only classification is the type, which sets the colour. Eight
types, and new events start as **Meeting**.

| Label | Colour | Typically used for |
|---|---|---|
| **Holiday** | Red | Nursery closures and public holidays |
| **Trip** | Blue | Outings and visits |
| **Performance** | Violet | Shows, concerts, graduations |
| **Meeting** | Amber | Parents' evenings, staff meetings — **the default** |
| **Class** | Green | Class-specific sessions |
| **Assessment** | Orange | Assessment periods and reporting deadlines |
| **Reminder** | Pink | Prompts and deadlines |
| **Other** | Grey | Anything that does not fit the rest |

**When they appear:** Chosen from the **Event type** dropdown when creating or editing an
event. Not required — it defaults to **Meeting**.
**What happens next:** The type sets the colour on the month grid, the agenda badge and the
event dialog, and appears in the **Event types:** legend. **That is all it does** — it sends no
reminders and notifies nobody.
**Do you need to do anything?** No, though choosing the right type makes the month grid far
easier to read at a glance.
**Worth knowing:** If parents need to know about an event, tell them through **Messages** —
putting it on the calendar does not reach them. And there are no recurring events, so a weekly
fixture needs an entry for each occurrence.

---

## Message states

Shown as a dot on each item in the **Messages** history.

### Published
**What it means:** The post is live on the parents' timeline.
**When it appears:** When you publish a post.
**Colour:** Green dot.
**What happens next:** It appears on the families' timeline. If it is a pinned notification, it
can also surface on the **Live Dashboard** in **Upcoming & Pinned**.
**Do you need to do anything?** No.

### Unpublished
**What it means:** The post is saved but not visible to parents.
**When it appears:** When you save without publishing, or schedule a post for later.
**Colour:** Amber dot.
**What happens next:** Nothing until you publish it.
**Do you need to do anything?** Yes — publish it when it is ready. Use the **Unpublished**
toggle above the history to find anything waiting.

**Not a status:** there are no read receipts. The portal cannot tell you whether a parent opened
a post. The dot shows **your** publishing state, not whether anybody read it.

---

## Message types

Six types, each with its own colour, chosen from the tab strip.

| Label | Colour | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| **Announcement** | Amber | General news to families |
| **Notification** | Blue | Alerts and important notices; the only type that can be pinned |
| **Event** | Violet | An event with start and end dates |
| **Newsletter** | Green | A newsletter document |
| **Media** | Pink | Photos |
| **Video Media** | Red | A video |

**Worth knowing:** clicking a type tab filters the history **and** opens the compose dialog at
the same time. If you only meant to filter, press Escape or **Cancel** — the filter stays
applied.

You may also see **Daily report** items in cyan in the history. These arrive automatically when
daily reports are published; there is no tab for creating one here.

---

## Attainment levels

**Attainment levels are created by your nursery**, so the names on your screen are your own. The
portal ships with no built-in scale.

Each level carries a name, a mark that sets its order (lowest to highest), a colour, and a
description.

**Where you see them:** the **Attainment** page, and the rating dropdowns on **Observations**.
**When they appear:** In the dropdown against each objective you tag on an observation.
**What happens next:** The rating is stored with the observation and feeds the assessment
report.
**Do you need to do anything?** **Yes — every objective you tag must be given an attainment
level** before the observation will save. Setting the **Age Group Attainment** dropdown at the
top first gives each objective a default rating as you tick it, which saves a lot of clicking.
**For whoever sets them up:** create your levels **before** teachers start observing — without
them the rating dropdown is empty and no observation can be saved. Fill in the **Description**
for each level, since that is the text teachers choose from. And give each level a distinct
mark, because that number sets the order.

---

## Labels that look like statuses but are not

A few coloured pills look like states and are not. Worth recognising so you do not read meaning
into them.

**INV** — The sky-blue badge on every invoice card. A document label meaning "this is an
invoice", identical on every row. It says nothing about payment. *Where:* Invoices.

**The receipt badge** — The amber badge on a receipt card shows the **type** of receipt
document. There is no cleared or pending concept. Worth knowing: a cheque recorded on a receipt
counts as money received straight away, whatever its clearing date. Where no type has been set,
it reads **RV**. *Where:* Receipts.

**Service Type** — A catalogue classification on a service — **Services**, **Resources**,
**Inventory** or **Expense**. It describes what kind of item it is, not a state. *Where:*
Services.

**Tax Exempt** — A green **Yes** or red **No**, marking whether an item carries VAT. A setting,
not a status. On **Purchases**, set this toggle **before** entering lines, since changing it
afterwards refreshes the service list and clears the lines. *Where:* Services; Invoices;
Purchases.

**Balance colours** — On **Balance by Class**, **Billed** is always green with a `+`,
**Received** always red with a `-`, and **Balance** carries an `=`. **A family who owes you
money shows in grey; a family who has overpaid shows in red.** All figures are displayed
without a minus sign, so the colour and the prefix carry the direction. Read it deliberately —
**grey means owing, red means in credit.** *Where:* Balance by Class.

**Attachment badges** — On **Expenses**, small badges label the kind of file attached — Image,
PDF, Document, Sheet and so on — or **No file** where nothing has been attached. File labels,
not states. *Where:* Expenses.

**Trace Checks** — A **Yes** or **No** setting on a payment mode. A configuration option, not a
status. *Where:* Payment Modes.

**The Live pill** — The small green pill on the **Live Dashboard** header is decoration. It is
always shown and is not a connection indicator. *Where:* Live Dashboard.

**Popular** — The badge on one of the package cards is presentation rather than a
recommendation. *Where:* Packages.

**No statuses at all in finance.** Suppliers, Expenses, Expense Types, Payment Vouchers,
Purchases and Services carry no active/inactive setting and no approval state. And invoices
have no paid or unpaid marker — **Balance by Class** is how you see where a family stands.

---

## Quick lookup table

| Label | Where you see it | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| **Present** | Children Attendance; Staff Attendance | In today, checked in |
| **Absent** | Children Attendance; Staff Attendance | Did not attend, not arranged |
| **Late** | Children Attendance; Staff Attendance | Here, but arrived after the start |
| **Excused** | Children Attendance | Authorised absence — **children only** |
| **On Leave** / **Leave** | Staff Attendance | Authorised time off — **staff only** |
| **Not Marked** | Children Attendance; Staff Attendance | Nobody has recorded anything yet — not the same as absent |
| **P** | Attendance Reports | Present that day |
| **A** | Attendance Reports | Absent that day |
| **L** | Attendance Reports | Late that day |
| **V** | Attendance Reports | On leave — staff rows |
| **E** | Attendance Reports | Excused — children's rows |
| **–** | Attendance Reports | Not marked that day |
| **Active** | Most pages | In use, appears in dropdowns |
| **Inactive** | Most pages | Kept for history, out of everyday use |
| **Not Active** | My Center sessions | Same as Inactive |
| **New Lead** | Leads | A new enquiry nobody has worked yet |
| **Book a Tour** | Leads | A visit is being arranged; it has not happened yet |
| **KYC** | Leads | Gathering and checking the family's details |
| **Evaluation** | Leads | Assessing readiness and issuing the offer |
| **Admission** | Leads | The family is applying; paperwork in progress |
| **Accepted** | Leads | You have offered a place — **now create the child record** |
| **Registered** | Leads | Registration complete; the end of the enquiry |
| **Lost** | Leads | The family withdrew or went elsewhere |
| **Rejected** | Leads | The nursery declined the application |
| **Published** | Daily Reports | Report marked finished — **does not email anybody** |
| **Draft** | Daily Reports | Saved but not finished; parents cannot see it |
| **Report not created** | Daily Reports | Nothing written for this child today |
| **Parent has not viewed yet** | Daily Reports | A fixed line, not a read receipt |
| **Great / Good / Okay / Unsettled / Upset** | Daily Reports | The child's mood — choose one deliberately |
| **All / Most / Some / None** | Daily Reports | How much of a meal was eaten — add a comment too |
| **Slept Well / Brief Nap / No Nap / N/A** | Daily Reports | Nap record — **always fill in the times** |
| **Yes / No** (Items Needed) | Daily Reports | Whether to send more diapers, clothes, wipes or cream |
| **Holiday / Trip / Performance / Meeting / Class / Assessment / Reminder / Other** | Calendar | Event type; sets the colour only |
| **Published** (green dot) | Messages | Live on the parents' timeline |
| **Unpublished** (amber dot) | Messages | Saved but parents cannot see it |
| **INV** | Invoices | A document label, not a payment status |
| **RV** | Receipts | Shown when no receipt type was set |
| **Tax Exempt: Yes / No** | Services; Invoices; Purchases | Whether VAT applies |
| **Billed** (green, `+`) | Balance by Class | Total invoiced for that child |
| **Received** (red, `-`) | Balance by Class | Total received from that family |
| **Balance** (`=`) | Balance by Class | **Grey means they owe you; red means they are in credit** |
| **No file** | Expenses | No receipt scan attached |
| **Trace Checks: Yes / No** | Payment Modes | A setting on the payment mode |


---


<!-- ================= 08-all-links ================= -->

# Every Page, One Click Away

Clicking any link below opens the portal at that page. If you have not used the portal yet
today, you may be asked to sign in first — sign in as usual and the page will open.

Two things worth knowing before you start. First, **your sidebar only lists the pages your
role is allowed to open**, so some links below belong to a colleague's job rather than
yours. If you open one that is not yours, you will see a short **Access denied** panel.
That is the account working exactly as intended, and your sign-in is unaffected — use the
back button, or the **Peekaboo logo** in the top bar, to return to the **Live Dashboard**.
Second, these links are worth saving as browser bookmarks (a saved shortcut to a page) for
the two or three pages you use every day.

---

## Signing in

| Page | What it is for | Who normally uses it |
|---|---|---|
| [Sign in](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/login) | The front door. Type your username and password, complete the **"I'm not a robot"** check, then click **Sign In**. | Everyone, at the start of each shift. |

---

## Operations — the pages the day runs on

### [Dashboard](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app)
The summary screen you land on after signing in. It shows today's headline numbers for
attendance, money and enquiries. Nothing here can be edited.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal and the office team, first thing each morning.

### [Children Attendance](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/attendance)
The morning register for children — **Present**, **Absent**, **Late** or **Excused** — and
where you check a child out at home time. It works on today only.
**Who normally uses it:** teachers, room leads and assistants, and reception.

### [Staff Attendance](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/staff-attendance)
The clock-in sheet for employees — **Present**, **Absent**, **Late** or **On Leave**. It
also works on today only.
**Who normally uses it:** room leads, the principal, and whoever runs the staff register.

### [Attendance Reports](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/attendance-reports)
The attendance history for any past date range, for children or for staff. You read it
rather than change it.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal, the office, and anyone answering an inspection
or a parent's question about past attendance.

### [Daily Reports](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/daily-reports)
The care record that goes home to the family — mood, meals, naps, nappies, items needed
and the **Note to parents**.
**Who normally uses it:** teachers, room leads and assistants, across the day.

### [Calendar](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/calendar)
The nursery diary — holidays, trips, performances, meetings and key dates.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal, registrars and room leads.

---

## People — the two rosters

### [Children](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/children)
Every child on your books, and behind each one their parent details, health records,
authorised pickup people and class registrations. Everything else in the portal points
back to this page.
**Who normally uses it:** registrars and principals to create and edit records; teachers
and assistants to look up allergies and pickup people.

### [Staff](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/staff)
The employee register, and where a colleague's portal login and role are created.
**Who normally uses it:** the administrator and the principal.

---

## Communication

### [Messages](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/messages)
The nursery's noticeboard to families. You publish announcements, newsletters, photos and
videos to selected children, and they reach parents in the separate parent app. It is
one-way; parents cannot reply here.
**Who normally uses it:** the office, the principal and room staff sharing photos.

---

## Your nursery's own settings

### [My Center](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/my-center)
Your nursery's own details — name, address, phone numbers, email, logo, VAT details — and
the **Sessions** tab where your daily time slots are set. Anything about *our* nursery is
here.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal or the office administrator, a few times a year.

### [Centers](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/centers)
The multi-nursery screen, where a brand-new nursery on the platform is set up. This is a
different page from **My Center**.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal alone, and rarely.

### [Packages](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/packages)
Your nursery's own subscription to the Peekaboo software. This is not parent billing and
never appears on a family's invoice.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal or owner.

---

## Early Childhood — curriculum, observations and assessment

### [Classes](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/classes)
Your actual rooms — name, capacity, age group, the teachers assigned to each, and what
appears on that room's daily report.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal or office administrator, at setup and when
staffing changes.

### [Curriculum](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/curriculum)
The learning framework your nursery assesses against, and the top of the early-years
structure. Everything else in this group belongs to a curriculum.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal and the curriculum lead, at setup.

### [Age Groups](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/age-groups)
The age bands your nursery uses, each defined by a range in months.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal and the curriculum lead, at setup.

### [Attainment](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/attainment)
Your grading scale — the levels a teacher picks from when rating how securely a child has
grasped an objective.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal and the curriculum lead, at setup.

### [Learn Area](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/learning-area)
The broad areas of learning within your curriculum, and the narrower **Aspects** inside
each one.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal and the curriculum lead, at setup.

### [Assessments](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/assessment)
A small summary page with three counters and links through to the two pages where the
work actually happens.
**Who normally uses it:** principals and curriculum leads, for a quick overview.

### [Observations](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/observation)
Where a teacher records what they saw a child do, and tags it against the objectives it
demonstrates.
**Who normally uses it:** teachers and room leads, through the week.

### [Assessment Report](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/early-childhood/assessment-report)
The per-child, per-learning-area printable summary of progress that parents receive at
the end of a term.
**Who normally uses it:** teachers, room leads and principals, at the end of each term.

---

## Finance — money going out

### [Suppliers](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/suppliers)
The address book of everyone you buy from or pay out to.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant or office administrator.

### [Expenses](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/expenses)
A cost the nursery has incurred, with the receipt or invoice scanned and attached.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant.

### [Expense Types](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/expense-types)
Your spending categories — Cleaning, Food, Stationery, Utilities, Maintenance.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant or administrator, at setup.

### [Payment Voucher](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/payment-voucher)
Money actually leaving the nursery to a supplier, split across one or more payment lines.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant.

### [Purchases](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/purchases)
What was ordered from a supplier, itemised line by line with quantities, fees and VAT.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant or office administrator.

---

## Account — money coming in

### [Services](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/services)
The catalogue of everything you charge for — tuition, transport, uniform — each with a
default fee and VAT treatment. Set this up before invoicing.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant, principal or administrator, at setup.

### [Invoices](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/invoices)
Billing a named child's family for one or more services.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant, and sometimes the registrar or principal.

### [Receipts](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/receipts)
Recording money received from a family.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant, and reception when taking a payment.

### [Payment Modes](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/payment-modes)
The ways money can move — cash, cheque, bank transfer, card — each mapped to an accounting
code. These must exist before a receipt or voucher can be saved.
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant or administrator, at setup.

### [Balance by Class](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/balance-by-class)
The outstanding-fees view: everything **Billed** for a child, everything **Received** from
them, and the **Balance** between the two. This is the one page that answers "who owes us
money?".
**Who normally uses it:** the accountant and the principal.

---

## Reports & Admin

### [Attendance Reports](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/attendance-reports)
Listed here as well, because it sits in this sidebar group. The read-only attendance
history for any past date range.
**Who normally uses it:** the principal, the office and guest accounts such as inspectors.

### [Roles & Access](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/roles)
Where it is decided which parts of the portal each job role can open. Access is set per
role, never per person.
**Who normally uses it:** the system administrator alone.

---

## Admissions CRM — enquiries from new families

### [Leads](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/crm/leads)
Every enquiry the nursery has received, from the first phone call through a tour to
enrolment, with its statuses, notes and follow-up tasks. Shown in the sidebar as
**CRM Leads**.
**Who normally uses it:** the registrar and admissions staff, with the principal looking
in.

### [Referrals & Sources](https://peek.peek-a-boo.app/app/crm/referrals-sources)
The two lookup lists recording where enquiries come from — how the family heard about you,
and which channel the enquiry arrived through. Both lists must have entries, because every
enquiry needs one of each.
**Who normally uses it:** the system administrator alone. Registrars ask the administrator
to add a new entry.

---

*If a page you need is not in your sidebar, that page is not part of your role here. Ask
your administrator, who can adjust what your role can open. After they make the change,
sign out and sign back in to pick it up.*


---
